TEEFIED FLYING OR TEGTS IN WALES

4a We have had two recent letters from Mr Nichclas Edwards MP, the Member fo
Pembroke, about Local enguiries he has received on the subject of Unidentificd
Flying Objects. We have “al so had a number of letters direct from members of the
yublic in Wales (although not from residents of Pembroke) and in addition there
. have been one or two quite lurid prees reports. J attach copies of the relevant
£ correspondence, :

‘

2. We have not invoked the assistance of P & SS before on UFOs and as I
explained on the telephone the last thing I want to do is to involve you in
extraneeus problems which would divert you from your gore immediate work on low
flying complaints. The standard procedure on UFOs i Zi Send the reports to the
various specialist authorities in MOD to make sure they contain no items of defence
interest but to leave it there. We have followed the usual procedure in this case
and nothing has come to light to suggest enything untoward in Wales and in the
normal way we would have left it et that. I confess, however, thac I find the
. correspondence a little pugzling. According to the press cuttings some of the
"witnesses" are people who right be expected to be fairly level. headed and
Mr Edwards does say in his letter of 24th May that - ue been inundated with
enquiries (I should add however that the es is known to be associé
‘with a local UFO society and so may have a vested interest).

3 I showld be grateful if you would have a look at these papers and let ime know
whether you think some sert of discreet enquiry would be worthwhile and if so
whether your staff could find time for it in view of yeur other pressing obligatio:
What I would really like to know is the volume of local interest anc/or alarm and
whether there is a readily discernible rational explanation for it (perhaps a
practical joker) or even whether there is prima facie evidence “ie a@ more serious
specialist enquiry. But I have not comaitted.you' in anyway and I have not even

told the Minister I am consulting ou Gsker tpn *’ a P& S&S initiati ve is Likely
to be usffruitful I will drop the ¢ Ou please let me know what you think.

The CRO has visited Ee bet: he tells us that he can offer no
explanation for the phenomenthe believes she saw in April. She spoke I
understand of an object the size of a mini bus which descended into the
field and from which emerged tw humanoid figures. They had gone when she
returned with help. ‘The CRO inspected the field but there was ho trace of
any unusual visitation. There is a fair amount of activity in the general
area from the Royal Aircraft Zstablishment and also from civil aircraft
although neither of these is a likely explanztion. The CRO added that tnere
is some general speculation in the neighbourhood that a practical joker

may be at work. RAF Brawdy have had other enquiries about men in silver suits
and it is perhaps not irrelevant that a local factory manufactures protective
clothing of this tyve for the-oil installation at Milford Haven. One of
these "silver suits" is also on display ih a shop at Brawdy village. I
understand incidentally that RAF fire fighting suits (the nearest Service
equivalent) are blue and that there is no possibility Brawdy personnel are
involved. ROC uniforms are also blue.

Following our recent communication I have visited Mrs ee =: the i
a: Haverfordwest. who is of French/Spanigh extraction

an her early 50's told me that one night in April of this year she saw a
round object ‘like the moon falling down! land in a field at the back of her
.property. Two very tall, faceless 'humanoids' got out of this object (about
the size of a mini bus) and appeared to 'take measurements or gather things!

When HE <cternca after calling members of the family the objects
had vanished. .

I examined the sugvested landing site, which is in a field adjacent to a
Royal Ooserver Corn underground monitoring post (MOD Post Shelter?) but could
find no evidence of a landing. I can offer no explanation of

sighting but can confirm that it is not connected with the operations of
RAF Braywdy.

There have been mmerous sightings of UFO's in what the pomlar press now call
‘the Broad Haven Triangle'. A high level of flying activity is generated by

the military and civil airfields and to a lesser extent the Royal. Aircraft
Establishments; the area is also overflown by} trans-atlantic aircraft. It has
also been sugsested that a local vrankster is at work. The majority of sightings

are renorted to a i TD °° Roch who is the 'regional investigating
co-ordinator! for the British UFO Research Association. . .

I'm unable te offer any explanation other than above but should a UFO arrive
at RAF Brawdy ze will charge normal landing fees and inform you immediately!

Thank you for your letter of 24th May 7. 0Clti(‘<i‘CCCOC*‘*d the
enquiries you have received recently on the phenomena which are
thought to be Unidentified Flying Objects.

The Ministry of Defence has not the resources to conduct an
objective scientific enquiry into the nature of UFOs and we do not
undertake to identify objects that may be reperted by members of the
public. For obvious reasons, however, we look into detailed reports
of unexplained phenomena to see if they have a bearing on the na
defences. Hy Department have carried out that type of investigats:
on the reports received from Wales but these have been few in recent
none. No evidence has been revealed to suggest that the alleged.

ehtings in Wales, or indeed those reperted from other parts of
ira at Britain in the past, contained anything of significance from
a defence point of view. Ali I can tell you on the basis of the
Department's past experience is that most reports of this nature can
usually be referred to a common place object which may have beeu
observed perhaps from an unusual angle or in somewhat unusual
weather or lighting conditions.

I_am sorry I cannot give you a more helpful reply to pass to
who I understand isa'co-ordinator' for the British UFO
Research Association. 4

cm Letter from US of S(RAF) to Nicholas Edwards MP
Thank you for your ‘Letter of 2kth May about the enquiries you have received
- recently on the phenomena which are thought to be Unidentified Flying Objects.

; ‘

The Ministry of Defence has not the resources to conduct an objective scientific
enquiry into the nature of UFOs and we do not undertake to identify objects that
may be-reported by members of the public. For obvious reasons, however, we look .
into detailed reports of unexplained phenomena to see if they have a bearing on
the national defences. The Department have carried out that type of investigation
on the reports received from Wales but these have been few in recent months and
none has come from Pembrokeshire. No evidence has been revealed to suggest that
the alleged sightings in Wales, or indeed those reported from other parts of
-Great Britain in the past, contained anything of significance from a defence point
of view. All I can tell you on the basis of the Department's past experience is
‘that most reports of this nature can usually be referred to a common place object
which may have been observed perhaps from an unusual angle or in somewhat
unusual weather or lighting conditions. I hope this helps to reassure your

constituents.

The CRO has visited >: he tells us that he can offer no
explanation for the phenomenshe believes she saw in April. She spoke I
understand of an object the size of a mini bus which descended into the
field and from which emerged te humanoid figures. They had sons when she
returned with help. ‘The CRO inspected the field but there vas no trace of
any unusual visitation. There is a fair amount of activity in the general
area from the Royal Aircraft Establishment and also from civil aircraft
although neither of these is a likely explanation. ‘The CRO added that there
is some general speculation in the neighbourhood that a practical joker

may be at work. RAF Brawdy have had other enquiries about men in silver suits
and it is perhaps not irrelevant that a local factory manufactures protective
clothing of this type for the:oil installation at Milford Haven. One of
these "silver suits" is also on display ih a shop at Brawdy village. I
understand incidentally that RAP fire fighting suits (the nearest Service
equivalent) are blue and that there is no possibility Brawdy personnel are
involved. ROC uniforms are also blue.

Following our recent communication I have visited at the Ea
a, Haverfordwest. Et! who is of French/Spanieh extraction

and in her early 50's told me that one night in April of this year she saw a
round object 'like the moon falling down! land in a field at the back of her
.property. Two very tall, faceless 'humanoids' got out of this object (about
the size of a mini bus) and appeared to 'take measurements or gather things'.

When BE ceturned after callinz.members of the family the objects
had vanished. . SS ‘

I examined the suggested landing site, which is in a field adjacent to a
Royal Ooserver Corn underground monitoring post (MOD Post Shelter?) but could
find no evidence of a landing. I can offer no explanation of Mrs Granville's

sighting but can confirm that it is not connected with the overations of
RAF Brawdy.

There have been mmerous sightings of UFO's in what the pomlar press now call

‘the Broad Haven Triangle', A high level of flying activity is generated by

the military and civil airfields and to a lesser extent the Royal Aircraft
Establishments; the area is also overflown by trans-atlantic aircraft. It has

also been suggested that a local vrankster is at work. The majority of sightings

are revorted to a EL msronmrasmae of Roch who is the 'regional investigating
co-ordinator’ for the Britis! Research Association. - é

“I'm unable te offer any explanation other than above but should a UFO arrive
at RAP Brawdy we will charge normal landing fees and inform you immediately!

Dear Dr Clarke
Thank you for your letter of 8 January 2004.

1 apologise for not including copies of the notes of the HQ Provost and Security Services
(HQ P&SS) investigation into the sonic event near Sheffield on 24 March 1997, with my letter of
23 December 2003. These are now attached for your information.

With regard to your questions about whether HQ P&SS made enquiries with RAF Fylingdales
about these events, it may be useful if I explain that HQ P&SS investigate reported sonic events
to establish whether they could have been caused by military aircraft activity. Once it is
established that there is no military link {as in this case), they do not pursue their enquiries to the
point of positively identifying the cause. I can, therefore, confirm that they did not make any
enquiries with RAF Fylingdales. During the investigation, the British Geological Survey stated
that satellite or meteor re-entry have been known to cause sonic events and this comment was
included in the HQ P&SS report.

In order to assist with your enquiries, I asked RAF Fylingdales to check their records for any
details of satellite re-entries between 2150Z and 2230Z on 24 March 1997 and the following is the
information they have provided. Please note that RAF Fylingdales track satellites and space
debris and do not hold information on meteors, comets, etc.

“RAF Fylingdales have accurate information for all large object (> than 1 sq m) re-entries,
but have none for the day in question (or several days before or after).

Data on small object re-entries are not so accurate. We are provided with a decay date
(but no time) for these events, and the accuracy of these dates cannot be given high
credence (most are OK +/- 1 day).
Between 23-25 March 1997 we have details of two small object re-entries:

Object 24010 (94029BF) and 24497 (94029 UW)
These were fragments from a Pegasus rocket 700-piece break-up. Both fragments will
have been very small. Most from this break-up were thin shards of carbon-fibre wire a

few centimetres in length.

However, there is no evidence of anything that may cause a sonic boom”.

I hope this is helpful.

Yours sincerely,

Fyl do not hold any info on meteors, comets, etc. We have accurate info for all large object (>1 sq m) re-entries but
there were none for the day in question (or several days before or after).

Data on small object re-entries are not so accurate. We are provided with a decay date (but no time) for these
events, and the accuracy of these dates cannot be given high credence (most are probably OK +/- 1 day).

Between 23 - 25 March 97 we have details of two small object re-entries:
Object 24010 (94029 BF) and 24497 (94029 UW).

These were fragments from a pegasus rocket 700-piece break-up. Both fragments will have beén very small. Most
from this break-up were thin shards of carbon-fibre wire a few centimetres in length.

However, no evidence for anything that may cause a sonic boom.

23 December 2003

Dear Dr Clarke

Thank you for your letter of 26 November concerning MOD files relating to ‘unidentified flying
objects’ and your comments regarding public access to those files. It may assist you if I explain
the MOD’s position with regard to the records we hold and the release of those records in light of
Open Government Initiatives.

Government records which are not yet 30 years old, whether held in the National Archive or in
MOD archives and offices, are not generally open for public inspection and access to information
contained in them is granted at the discretion of the department to which the records belong. The
Open Government Initiatives, including the White Paper on Open Government published in July
1993 and the Code of Practice on Access to Government Information (the Code), do not affect
departmental discretion. In accordance with the Code, information will be released on request,
providing it is not subject to Code exemptions, the provisions of the Data Protection Act, or any

other relevant Act of Parliament. The Code will be superseded by the Freedom of Information
Act 2000 on ~
1 January 2005.

With regard to the granting of special access to selected individuals, this is inconsistent with the
spirit of open government. Access to original documents is, therefore, only approved if the
individual is involved in research which is commissioned, sponsored or approved by the MOD or
is seen to be research from which HM Government or MOD will derive benefit. As you will be
aware, the MOD has only a limited interest in ‘UFO’ matters and while it is entirely a matter for
you to decide the course of your research, we do not believe that your studies would provide
significant benefit to the MOD. That is not to say that we do not appreciate that there is a public
interest in this material and for that reason we have included classes of information on ‘UFO’s’ in
the MOD’s Freedom of Information Publication Scheme. It is also our intention to place
additional classes in the Scheme and we are currently examining our files in order to make any
release as comprehensive and logical as possible.
Turning now to your requests for an estimate of the cost of processing the files detailed in your
letter, in accordance with the Code. Please see the attached table. As requested, I have provided a
separate estimate for each of the numbered files/sets of files wherever possible. The estimate is
based on the number of pages requiring examination on each individual file and the cost of each
set is given in full. If you were to request these documents in batches, the first four hours of the
first batch would be free of charge, thereby reducing that batch by £60. With regard to file set
number 7 (shown as 6 in your letter), (D/DI55/108/15 Parts 4-7), these files are currently held in
an archive which is subject to asbestos contamination and we do not have access to them at this
time. It is impossible, therefore, to provide an estimate of the cost of processing. Also, the file at
number 10 (9 in your letter)(DRP(RAF)326/2 Pt A) contains 282 pages, only 14 of which concern
UFO matters. It appears that ‘UFO’ may have been added to the title of the file to highlight the
fact that there are UFO papers on the file rather than that the whole file is about UFOs. To
process the whole file would cost considerably more than just to process the UFO papers, so I
have included both estimates for your information.

You also enquired about the RAF Police Investigator’s notes requested in your previous letter
dated 14 June, which the RAF Police were experiencing difficulties in retrieving. I apologise for
the delay. These have now been retrieved and copies are enclosed. The final report may differ
slightly from the copy sent with our earlier letter which we suspect was an earlier draft. While
disclosing these papers the RAF Police have asked me to explain to you that civil police
investigations are subject to “public interest immunity” which protects the freedom of
communication between the Police and the Crown Prosecution Service. The military police have a
similar system and this means that Service police reports are normally only disclosed to an
accused’s Commanding Officer and a limited number of Service legal personnel. Having
examined these papers, however, the RAF Police have concluded that as there is no suspect, no
interview transcript or written statements, they will make an exception on this occasion and
disclose the papers to you.

Finally, you asked for clarification regarding information for which individuals are charged. The
Code states that Departments, Agencies and public bodies should make their own arrangements
for charging for information to take account of the additional costs to the Department of searching
for the requested information, and processing it for release. The MOD deems it reasonable to
conduct four hours work free of charge and after this time to make a charge of £15 per extra hour
(or part thereof) worked. The Publication Scheme is part of the forthcoming Freedom of
Information Act and is a means of informing the public what information is readily available and
how they may gain access to it. Once information has been released into the public domain it
therefore makes sense to add it to the Publication Scheme, something which is actively
encouraged by the Information Commissioner. I do, however, understand your concerns
regarding information for which individuals have paid a charge, and I have raised this issue with
the Directorate who have the policy lead on both the Code and the Freedom of Information Act.

T will write to you again in the new year about this matter. In the meantime, you may wish to take
into account the fact that we are conducting a review of our files and do plan to release as much
information as possible into the Publication Scheme in due course. Also, as you are probably
aware, while this Department is the focal point within the MOD for UFO correspondence, at least
two other branches have been involved in this subject and by examining all the available files, at
the same time, we will aim to make the classes of information as comprehensive as possible. This
is something that might not be achieved by looking at individual files in isolation.

I hope this is helpful.

Yours sincerely,

MOD (DAS- Sec 3) Date: 5 Dec 03

DISCLOSURE OF DFCIT REPORT ON ALLEGED SONIC EVENT

Reference:

A. E-mail from DAS-Sec 3, transmitted 1 Dec 03.
1. At Reference A, you asked if DFCIT would release a copy of the DFCIT file papers to Dr
Clarke, covering the investigation into an alleged sonic boom occurring in the Peak District near

Sheffield on 24 Mar 97.

2. As you know we were experiencing difficulties in being able to retrieve information from
Scanfile, and having found a floppy disc containing the final report, we forwarded an unsigned copy
to you which, we understand was duly forwarded, in a suitably redacted manner, to Dr Clarke.

3. We have now retrieved the case file papers from Scanfile. Following advice from both the
HQ P&SS, Data Protection Officer (DPO) and Legal Advisor (LA), DFCIT are content for you to
release a copy to Dr Clarke. Two suitably redacted copies are attached, one for your retention. You
will note that the covering report is slightly different to the one retrieved from the floppy disc. We
suspect that the disc copy was the original draft.

4. For your background information, please be aware that in the civil system “public interest
immunity” attaches to a report sent from the police to the CPS. The rational behind such “public
interest immunity” is that it is important for the proper functioning of the criminal process that there
should be freedom of communication between the police and the CPS. Such a principle applies in
the military investigative system as well. That is to say that public interest immunity should be
considered to apply to service police reports and that they should only be disclosed to an accused’s
Commanding Officer, the Command Legal Cell, Higher Authority and the Royal Air Force
Prosecuting Authority. No other service personnel or department should be supplied with a copy of
the service police report.

5. ° Having considered the contents of the DFCIT file papers, both DPO and LA are of the view
that to rely on “public interest immunity” defence would be difficult in that the DFCIT report does
not appear to be a traditional police report. By this we mean there is no suspect as such and there is
no interview transcript or written statements taken in accordance with S9 of the Criminal and Justice
Act 1967.

ALLEGED SONIC EVENT - 24 MAR 97- SHEFFIELD, PEAK DISTRICT- FINAL REPORT

1. D/Sec(AS)/56/1 dated 25 Mar 98 tasked FCF with the investigation of an alleged sonic event
which was reported to have happened on 24 Mar 97 in the Peak district near Sheffield,

2” Alfens Ops records revealed that there were no military fast jet aircraft booked into the night

low flying area at the relevant times. Inquiries with all flying units failed to identify the ac concerned .

3. Mr. . -, British Geological Survey (BGS), Edinburgh stated that on the 24 Mar 97 at
2152Z seismic monitors had recorded a signal consistent with a sonic event. A further signal
consistent with a sonic event were recorded at 22062. These signals were monitored as coming from
the area reported as the complaint location at the monitoring stations at Haverah Park (south west of
Huddersfield) and. Holmfirth (south west of Leeds), A low frequency microphone at Leeds University
also recorded the sonic event. Mr’ 1 stated that although BGS can differentiate between sonic and
seismic events, sonic events cannot be identified as being specifically caused by aircraft. Phenomena

such as satellite or meteor re-entry have been known to cause sonic events and this may explain the
happenings of 24 Mar 97, : ,

4. AIS (Mil) at West Drayton were requested to provide a radar synopsis (Enclosure 1) and the
radar tape for the 24 Mar 97 was still available. The tape was viewed between 242130ZMar97 and
242200ZMar97, using the Claxby and Great Dun Fell Radar Heads. The recording reveals that at
21392, a sqwuak of 2574/143(Orcam/Karlshrue), is in descent heading west, At 2145 ac squawk
2574/048 passes to the north by 2 nm of the plotted position and the aircraft lands at Manchester
Airport at 2154Z. The Air Information Service Officer is of the opinion that this aircraft is too slow
moving to have caused a sonic boom. No other aircraft were seen to be flying in the area,

Dew SEEN

Thankyou for your letters of 28 July and 28 August in response to my requests for UFO-
related information under the Code of Practice. Further to my request for a copy of the
investigation file relating to the alleged sonic event near Sheffield on 24 March 1997, I
note that you are in the process of retrieving the notes made by the RAF police. Has there
been any progress in obtaining a copy of these notes?

eae jabee

Inow turn to your letter of 28 July in response to my offer of assistance towards your on-
going review of UFO records, and my request for access to MOD archives as part of my
postgraduate research application. I do of course appreciate the logistical and security
problems that would be entailed in involving non-MOD personnel access to sites where
records are filed. I also appreciate the fact that many contain correspondence subject to
the Data Protection Act and other sensitive information.

However, I do not accept these reasons are sufficient ones to deny access to public .
records — already earmarked for eventual release - by a qualified historian who is working faance
on a research project accredited by a leading UK university. I would point out that many rom
other academic projects deal with source material that is subject to Data Protection
legislation and university researchers are as equally bound by that legislation as are MOD
personnel.

I would also point out that the Department of Constitutional Affairs own guidance on
handling requests under the Code of Practice states that even when exemptions under the
Data Protection Act and national security apply “there is a presumption of disclosure
unless the likely harm would outweigh the public interest in making the information
available.”

Given the MOD’s public commitment to the release of UFO material and the impending
FOIA I believe the Parliamentary Ombudsman may take a different view of this matter. I
would therefore appreciate details of the procedure I should take should I wish to appeal.
Hon

“ In the meantime, I wish to thankyou for the list of UFO-related records held by MOD
from 1976-present. Having scrutinised this comprehensive list in some detail I note that
only a limited number of files are directly of interest to my research proposal. If it were
possible to obtain copies of these via one application under the Code of Practice it may
not be necessary to make any further requests.

Ihave listed the files that are of specific interest below. I would appreciate it if you could
supply a quote for processing them under the Code of Practice for Access to Government
Information. It would be helpful if you could provide a separate estimate for each of the
numbered file/set of files listed below:

D/Sec(AS)12/1 Pt A — UFO Policy 1985

D/Sec(AS)64/1 Pts A-E (5 files) — UFO Policy 1995-2002

1995-Dec 2002

9 DRP (RAF) 326/2 Pt A — Security Matters - Correspondence UFOs, 2000.

Finally, I would appreciate clarification of a point arising from the MOD Publication
Scheme. Currently, individual members of the public are charged at an hourly rate for the
copying and processing of files requested under the Code/FOIA. If those requested files,
duly processed, are then made available at a future date to other members of the public
via a separate personal request under the Code/FOIA will they also be charged at the
same rate?

Furthermore, what if the processed material is then, at some future date, made available

free of charge under your Publication Scheme? If that happens, should not the individual Dene?
who made the original request and originally paid for the processing of the file be entitled °
to a refund?

JSP 441 - Chapter 9 - Access to Records under 30 Page 1 of 2

Undatehe Last JSP Defence Records
@ Tuesday, 07 October Management Manual
2003 10:32 441 Chapter 9
Queries, Public Access to Records less
Suggestions, etc. than 30 years old

9.1 Criteria for Access
9.2 Sponsored Access

_ 9.1 Criteria for Access

9.1.1 Accéss to records which are not yet 30 years old, whether in the Public Record Office (PRO) or
held by MOD, is granted at the discretion of the government department to which the records belong.
The Open Government Initiatives, including the White Paper on Open Government published in July
1993 and the Code of Practice on Access to Government Information, do not affect departmental
discretion. They do, however, seek to reduce the amount of material which is closed for longer than
30 years and make provision for more records to be released by:

e use of more exacting criteria to justify continued closure;

e re-review of previously withheld information;

identification of material which is less than 30 years old but may be suitable for accelerated
release;

e response to ad hoc requests.

9.1.2 In addition, information and, in certain circumstances, documents, can be made available in
response to requests under the Code of Practice on Access to Government Information.

9.1.3 The guiding principle in these initiatives is to make as much as possible of the original record
available to the public. On receipt of requests for access to material before it is 30 years old MOD is
required to consider whether the material is suitable for early release. If it is the Lord Chancellor's
approval must be sought. If it is not suitable for release, or resources are not available for the process
of transfer to the PRO, MOD must consider whether granting access can be justified and defended as
being in the public interest.

9.2 Sponsored Access

9.2.1 "Privileged access", meaning the granting of special access to selected individuals, is
inconsistent with the spirit of open government. Access by individuals should therefore only
normally be approved if the individual is involved in research which is commissioned, sponsored or
approved by MOD or is seen to be research from which HMG or MOD will derive benefit, and can
be justified as being in the public interest. Such "sponsored access’ can be agreed at the discretion of
the business unit holding the records concerned.

9.2.2 Such access should only be approved if the work involved in making the appropriate records
available does not detract significantly from the ongoing work of the business unit.

9.2.3 In cases of doubt about the merit of allowing sponsored access, or for more detailed advice on

http://www.defence.mod.uk/drweb/JSP/Chapter%209.htm 05/12/03
JSP 441 - Chapter 9 - Access to Records under 30 Page 2 of 2

the subject, please contact DG Info-Records1.

Dear Dr Clarke

Further to my letter of 28 July 2003, please find enclosed a copy of the RAF Police report into an
alleged sonic event near Sheffield on 24 March 1997, as requested in your letter of 14 June 2003.
Personal data has been removed in accordance with the Data Protection Act 1998.

With regard to your request for the full investigation file into this incident, there is no actual
investigation file, but the investigator’s notes which would have been used to compile this report
are held electronically. The RAF Police are currently experiencing some difficulties with
retrieving this information which they hope will be solved shortly. I will write to you again as
soon as possible concerning this matter.

1. D/Sec(AS)/S6/1 dated 25 Mar 98 tasked FCF with the investigation of an alleged sonic event
which was reported to have happened on 24 Mar 97 in the Peak district near Sheffield.

2. Asearch of TBC records from archives revealed that there was no military fast jt aircraft booked
into the relevant night low flying area at the relevant times.

3. QIN o£ The British Geological Survey in Edinburgh stated that on the 24 Mar 97 at
2152Z his seismic monitors had recorded a signal consistent with a sonic event. A further signal
consistent with a sonic event were recorded at 2206Z. These signals were monitored as coming from
tbe area reported as the complaint location and monitoring stations at Haverah Park, south west of
Huddersfield, Holmfirth, south west of Leeds and alow frequency microphone at Leeds University
all recorded the sonic event. QMMPstated that although BGS can differentiate between sonic and
seismic events, sonic events can not be identified as being specifically caused by aircraft as other
phenomena such as satellite or meteor re-entry have been known to cause sonic events and this may
explain the happenings of 24 Mar 97.

4. AIS (Mil) at West Drayton were tasked with providing radar evidence and by pure coincidence
the radar tape for the 24 Mar 97 was still in existence having already been impounded for another
unconnected investigation. The tape was viewed between 242130ZMar97_ antl 242200ZMar97,
using the Claxby and Great Dun Fell radar heads. The recording reveals that at 2139Z, a sqwuak of
2574/143(Orcam/Karlshrue) in descent heading west. At 2145 : 09Z 2574/048 passes to the north by
2 mm of the plotted position and the aircraft lands at Manchester at 2154Z. The Air Information
Service Officer is of the opinion that this aircraft appears to be too slow moving to cause a sonic
boom and no other aircraft were seen to be flying in the area.

28 July 2003

Dear Dr Clarke

Thank you for your letter of 14 June concerning your request to assist with the review of UFO
material and your further requests for information. T apologise for the delay in answering your
letter, but I have tried to gather as much of the information you requested as possible before
replying.

We understand your interest in the future release of UFO material and I can assure you that we are
looking carefully at ways to make as much material as possible available to the public. However,
there are practical difficulties with involving non-MOD staff in this process. For example, the
files are not held at one location, but spread between The National Archive (previously the Public
Record Office), the MOD Records department, several MOD Archives and in the case of more
recent files, in more than one MOD office. I hope you will appreciate the logistical and security
problems with allowing non-MOD personnel access to all of these sites.

Then there is the matter of the contents of the files themselves. Many contain correspondence and
reports from people whose confidentiality we have a duty to protect under the Data Protection Act
1998. In addition, some may also contain sensitive information which the MOD may be required
to withhold under an exemption of the Code. As well as the obvious security issues of allowing
access to such information, we believe MOD staff are in the best position to judge such
sensitivities.

With regard to your comments about the need to put released material into historical context, it
may be helpful if I explain that under the Code of Practice on Access to Government Information
(the Code) and the forthcoming Freedom of Information Act (FON 2000, the MOD is required to
make requested information available wherever possible and, in the case of the FOI Act, to also
operate a Publication Scheme to inform members of the public what information is published and
how they can obtain access to it. We are not obliged to produce narratives, only to make
information available so that individuals can conduct their own research.

We appreciate your offer of assistance, but given the above, we believe that we are able to
conduct the review and release of future UFO information, and fulfil the requirements of the Code
and the FOI Act without external assistance.
t

eo" your letter you also asked if we would be able to notify you in advance of any plans to release
additional UFO material. It is not our practice to give any member of the public advanced
notification before any other.

Turning to your four new requests for information, I will address these in the same order as your

letter.

1,

As requested, please find enclosed a list of UFO-related records held by MOD, 1976 to
present, including the MOD file references, titles, date of contents and where appropriate
The National Archive reference. Names of members of the public mentioned in the titles
of some files have been excluded in accordance with the Data Protection Act 1998.

Our records show that in June 1977, following two letters from Mr Nicholas Edwards MP
and several UFO sighting reports from members of the public in Wales, the RAF Police
were asked to consider whether they thought it would be worthwhile for them to conduct
enquiries in order to gauge local interest and to see if there was a rational explanation for
these reports. There is no record on our files as to whether the RAF Police made any such
enquiries and the RAF Police do not retain records of correspondence for more than 7
years. I am therefore unable to supply the information you have requested here.

’ ?
Please see attached a copy of a letter from US of S for Defence (James Wellbeloved MP)
to Nicholas Edwards MP in response to a Parliamentary Enquiry about UFO reports in
Pembrokeshire. Although this letter is dated 16" June 1977 and not the 15" June as stated
in your letter, I believe this is one you have referred to. I also include a copy of the
background note by S4(Air) and draft reply as requested. Although not requested, for
completeness, I have also included a copy of Nicholas Edwards MP letter to

Dr John Gilbert MP as mentioned in the above letter. Personal data concerning members
of the public has been removed in accordance with the Data Protection Act 1998.

Details of this request have been passed to the RAF Police and I will write to you again
about this.

Finally, with regard to your enquiry about a clergyman’s letter to the Chief of the Defence Staff in
1977 requested in your last correspondence, we have not yet located this document, but if it
should come to light in the near future, we will of course send you a copy.

Thope this is helpful. I will write to you again as soon as possible regarding your fourth request.

Yours sincerely,

Thank you for your letter of 24th May to John Gilbert about the
enouiries you have received recently on the phenomena vhich are
thought to be Unidentified Flying Objects.

The Ministry of Defence has not the resources to conduct an
objective scientific enquiry into the nature of UFOs and we do not
undertake to identify objects that may be reperted by members of the
public. For obvious reasons, however. we look into detailed report
of unexplained phenomena to see if they have a bearing on the n: ]
defences. My Department have carried out that type of investigation
on the reports received frow Wales but these have been few in recent
months. No evidence has been revealed to suggest that the alleged
sightings in Wales, or indeed those reported from other parts of
Great Britain in the past, contained anything of significance from
a defence point of view. All I can tell you on the basis of the
Department's past experience is that most reports of this nature can
usually be referred to a common place object which may have beer
observed perbaps frem an unusual angle or in somewhat unusval
weather or lighting conditions.

“> DRAFT

c@- fron US of S(RAF) to Nicholas Edvards MP

Thank you for your letter of 2kth May, about the enquiries you have received
- recently on the phenomena which are thought to be Unidentified Flying Objects.
: Ul

The Ministry of Defence has not the resources to conduct an objective scientific
enquiry into the nature of UFOs and we do not undertake to.identify objects that
may be reported by members of the public. For obvious reasons, however, we leok
into detailed reports of unexplained phenomena to see if they have a bearing on
the national defences.. The Department have carried out that type of investigation
on the reports received from Wales but these have been few in recent months and
none has come from Pembrokeshire. No evidence has been revealed to suggest that
the alleged sightings in Wales, or indeed those reported from other parts of
-Great Britain in the past, contained anything of significance from a defence point
of view. All I can tell you on the basis of the Department's past experience is
‘that most reports of this nature can usually be referred to a common place object
which may have been ohserved perhaps from an unusual angle or in somewhat

unusual weather or lighting conditions. I hope this helps to reassure your

constituents.

Haven Fort Hotel

Settlands Hill, Little Haven, Haverfordwest, Pembrokeshire, South Wales, SA62 3LA
Telephone: 01437 781401

A uniquely attractive cliff top hotel, offering unsurpassed, breathtakingly beautiful views of St Bride
Bay and the coastal national park. The hotel, standing in a magnificent position in its own grounds,
dominates the picturesque village of Little Haven and commands superb panoramic views, from all

angles of the hotel. Safe bathing can be enjoyed at Settlands, the hotels own beach, the whole area is
renowned for its scenic beauty.

http://www.s-h-systems.co.uk/hotels/havenfort.html

11/07/2003
Haven Fort Hotel, Haverfordwest, Pembrokeshire Page 2 of 3

The national park offers something for everyone. There are miles of cliff top pathways for the walkei
offering views of unparalleled scenic splendour. The bays of Settlands. Little Haven and Broad Have
with their fine beaches are excellent for bathing, fishing, sailing, skin diving and surfing.

The hotel being of neo-Gothic architecture is fitted and furnished in the style of the
medieval period.

There are 15 letting bedrooms with private bathrooms.

The restaurant and bar are open to non-residents. The restaurant caters for English and
continental cooking of a high standard in beautiful surroundings: -

The hotel has been prominently featured in the books "The Welsh Triangle", "The Dyfed
Enigma", and "The Uninvited".

Open Easter - October.

Basic Tariff

Single bed and breakfast from £35.00
Double bed and breakfast from £55.00
Family bed and breakfast from £70.00 - £78.00

http://www.s-h-systems.co.uk/hotels/havenfort.html 11/07/2003

D/DAS/64/3/11

3 July 2003

REQUEST FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION UNDER THE CODE OF PRACTICE
ON ACCESS TO GOVERNMENT INFORMATION

1. Further to my telephone conversation with Flight Lieutenant BEaIOm OME earlier
today, I would be grateful for your assistance with the following request for information.

2. As you may be aware, this section of DAS deals with correspondence concerning
‘unidentified flying objects’ (UFOs). I attach a copy ofa letter we have received from one of
our regular correspondents, Dr David Clarke, who has made several requests for information in
accordance with the Code of Practice on Access to Government Information (the Code). At
request number 4 and in the following paragraph he has requested a copy of an RAF Police
report and the full investigation file concerning a sonic boom near Sheffield, Yorkshire on
24 March 1997. Dr Clarke has already been told the outcome of the investigation, namely,
that no RAF or NATO aircraft were operating in the area at the time and that civilian aircraft in
the area were travelling too slow to have generated a sonic boom.

3. Please could you locate the records requested by Dr Clarke and advise me as to whether
they can be released.

4. Also attached for your information, is some details of Dr Clarke’s interests in
‘Unidentified Flying Objects’, taken from his website www. flyingsaucery.com.

5. If you require any further information, please give me a call. Dr Clarke will be told that
this aspect of his requests has been passed to the RAF Police and we will write to him as soon
as possible.

DAS-LA-Ops+Poll

M19 SS

Dr David Clarke

Directorate Air Staff
Ministry of Defence
Metropole Building
Northumberland Avenue
London WC2N 5BP

14 June, 2003

Des: SEE

Thankyou for your letter of 24 March and for the copies of outstanding papers arising
from my request for UFO material under the Code of Practice. Since receiving your letter
T am reminded that you were searching for a copy of the original letter from the
clergyman to the Chief of Defence staff in 1977 that was the basis for my original
request. Has this letter been located?

With rererence to your point no. 2 in response to my offer of assistance towards your on-
going review of UFO records. As I pointed out, I am currently preparing an application to
the E.S.R.C., who are an academic research body recognised by the MOD, to undertake a
two-year project that will examine the development of public policy towards the UFO
problem. My proposal would seem to supplement both your review and your
commitment to make further UFO material available to the public under the publication
scheme .

Most importantly of all, it would provide vital historical context to primary documents
that may otherwise be fragmentary and possibly even misleading. For example, the
contents of file DS8/75/3 Part A relating to the preparation for the House of Lords
debate of 1979 would, if released in its present form be confusing, if not meaningless,
without the benefit of a introduction or a chronological index to assist members of the
public, The PRO, for instance, routinely publish on their website detailed introductions to
supplement their document releases that are prepared in advance by historians with an
established interest in the subject area.

As you note it will be a significant task for someone without a detailed knowledge of the
history of the subject to review and identify items hidden among the 250 plus files dating
back to the 1970s. My offer was made on the understanding that, as you have previously
stated, the Ministry has no special interest or expertise in respect of the UFO
phenomenon or its interpretation.

For these reasons I hope you will reconsider your decision not to take up my offer to
assist, and/or contribute, under the auspices of the E.S.R.C. The application form
requires me to indicate if I believe my proposed project would be of interest to MOD, and
urges applicants to approach the Ministry in advance of submission, hence my
correspondence.

In the meantime would you be able to notify me in advance of any plans to publish or
release additional UFO material — apart from the files routinely released at the PRO under
the 30 year rule - during the period of my research proposal (2004-6)?

I now wish to make four new applications for copies of material under the Code of
Practice for Access to Government Information. These are as follows:

1. A listing of UFO-related records held by MOD, 1976-present, including MOD
references and/or PRO references, with file titles and descriptions of contents
where available.

2. Copy of file containing RAF Police report into UFOs reported in the
Haverfordwest area of West Wales during the spring/summer of 1977. This may
be filed alongside a sighting report made to MOD by Mrs Rosa Grenville of the
Haven Fort Hotel, Pembrokeshire, on or about 19 April 1977.

3. Background briefing by S4 (Air) and copy of letter from the US of S for Defence
(James Wellbeloved MP) to Nicholas Edwards MP for Pembroke, dated 15 June
1977 in response to a Parliamentary Enquiry concerning reports of UFO incidents
made in his West Wales constituency.

4. Copy of RAF Police report on their investigation into reports of unidentified low
flying aircraft and sonic booms reported near Sheffield, Yorkshire, on 24 March
1997.

The existence of the report referred to in item (4) was confirmed in a letter I received
fromSBRieelll of Sec(AS)2a, dated 4 January 2000. He said although RAF police
reports were ‘classified’ he was able to provide a summary of its content. However, under
the Code I have already successfully applied for and received copies of RAF Police
reports with personal details deleted. As no RAF/NATO aircraft were identified during
the investigation and no further action taken I now wish to apply for a copy of the full
investigation file under the term of the Code.

Thank you for your attention to my requests.

Yours sincerel:
Flying Saucery a Frage | ors

INTERESTS

Dave and Andy are working on a number of other UFO and fortean projects and would be
interested in any information you may have on the following:

Flying Saucers & Unidentified Flying Objects

British contactees 1947-70/George Adamski in the UK
UFOlogy in the 1950s

'Death' rays and related phenomena

Phantom airship sightings of the late 19th/early 20th centuries

DAVID CLARKE

Ph.D in English Cultural Tradition and Language, University of Sheffield (1999)
Thesis subject was the cult of the human head in Celtic mythology, British tradition and folklore.

BA (Hons) dual honours Archaeology and Medieval History at Sheffield University (graduated
1990).

As a UFOlogist, I was a founder member of the Independent UFO Network (IUN) in 1987 and of
the UFO Investigator's Network (UFOIN) in 1999. I have also served as a Council member of the
British UFO Research Association (BUFORA) in the late 1980s.

As a journalist, I have worked for ten years on local newspapers including the Rotherham
Advertiser and Sheffield Star and now work freelance as a journalist and

full time as an author.

1 hold a Honorary Research Fellowship in the National Centre for English Cultural Tradition and
Language, University of Sheffield, where I teach Traditions of Supernatural Belief, a 2nd and 3rd
year undergraduate option in the School of English.

My specialist areas of research interest are:

@ contemporary legend and belief: specifically 'space age’ folklore (conspiracy
theories, 'flying saucer’ and UFO beliefs);

@ rumour transmission and rumour-panics within a supernatural context;
@ the interface between archaeology, oral tradition and belief;
© custom and belief, specifically in the context of northern England and the Peak District

Books:

Phantoms of the Sky: UFOs - A Modern Myth? (with Andy Roberts), London: Robert Hale, 1990.
Ghosts and Legends of the Peak District, Norwich: Jarrold Publishing, 1991.

Strange South Yorkshire: Myth, Magic and Memory in the valley of the Don, Wilmslow: Sigma
Press, 1994.

A Guide to Britain's Pagan Heritage, London: Robert Hale, 1995.

Twilight of the Celtic Gods: An exploration of Britain's hidden pagan traditions, London: Blandford
Press, 1996.

The UFOs that Never Were, London (with Jenny Randles and Andy Roberts), Allison & Busby,
1999.

Supernatural Peak District, London: Robert Hale, 2000.

Out of the Shadows
UFOs, the Establishment & the official Cover-up

Out of the Shadows: UFOs, the Establishment & the official Cover-
up is the new UFO book by David Clarke and Andy Roberts,
published by Piatkus in 2002.

Out of the Shadows takes a fresh look at the subject of UFOs, focussing upon the British
Ministry of Defence's investigations into the phenomena since 1940. Clarke and Roberts have
spent two years researching the book and have uncovered hundreds of previously unseen MOD
documents relating to UFO activity in the UK.

The book sets the MOD policy on UFOs alongside the beliefs and attitudes of the British UFO
community and the wider media towards both the subject and allegations of an official ‘cover up'.

Out of the Shadows is not a sceptical book, nor is it written for ‘believers.’ We see it as a social
history of the flying saucer/UFO phenomenon in the UK. It objectively traces the development of
official interest and investigations into UFOs and reaches some startling conclusions.

Among its many and varied contents, the book features:

@ Important new material on WWII UFO sightings and investigations
© Hitherto unseen Ministry of Defence and RAF files on UFO reports by service personnel.

e Interviews with and comments with numerous individuals who have worked at the highest
level of governmental UFO investigation

14 June, 2003

Thankyou for your letter of 24 March and for the copies of outstanding papers arising

from my request for UFO material under the Code of Practice. Since receiving your letter

I am reminded that you were searching for a copy of the original letter from the Dr cet vw
clergyman to the Chief of Defence staff in 1977 that was the basis for my original

request. Has this letter been located?

With reference to your point no. 2 in response to my offer of assistance towards your on-
going review of UFO records. As I pointed out, I am currently preparing an application to
the E.S.R.C., who are an academic research body recognised by the MOD, to undertake a
two-year project that will examine the development of public policy towards the UFO
problem. My proposal would seem to supplement both your review and your .
commitment to make further UFO material available to the public under the publication
scheme .

Most importantly of all, it would provide vital historical context to primary documents
that may otherwise be fragmentary and possibly even misleading. For example, the
contents of file DS8/75/3 Part A relating to the preparation for the House of Lords
debate of 1979 would, if released in its present form be confusing, if not meaningless,
without the benefit of a introduction or a chronological index to assist members of the
public. The PRO, for instance, routinely publish on their website detailed introductions to
supplement their document releases that are prepared in advance by historians with an
established interest in the subject area.

As you note it will be a significant task for someone without a detailed knowledge of the
history of the subject to review and identify items hidden among the 250 plus files dating
back to the 1970s. My offer was made on the understanding that, as you have previously
stated, the Ministry has no special interest or expertise in respect of the UFO
phenomenon or its interpretation.

For these reasons I hope you will reconsider your decision not to take up my offer to
assist, and/or contribute, under the auspices of the E.S.R.C. The application form
requires me to indicate if I believe my proposed project would be of interest to MOD, and
urges applicants to approach the Ministry in advance of submission, hence my
correspondence.

In the meantime would you be able to notify me in advance of any plans to publish or
release additional UFO material — apart from the files routinely released at the PRO under
the 30 year rule - during the:period of my research proposal (2004-6)?

I now wish to make four new applications for copies of material under the Code of
Practice for Access to Government Information. These are as follows:

1. A listing of UFO-related records held by MOD, 1976-present, including MOD
references and/or PRO references, with file titles and descriptions of contents
where available.

2. Copy of file containing RAF Police report into UFOs reported in the
Haverfordwest area of West Wales during the spring/summer of 1977. This may
be filed alongside a sighting report made to MOD by Mrs Rosa Grenville of the
Haven Fort Hotel, Pembrokeshire, on or about 19 April 1977.

3. Background briefing by S4 (Air) and copy of letter from the US of S for Defence
(James Wellbeloved MP) to Nicholas Edwards MP for Pembroke, dated 15 June
1977 in response to a Parliamentary Enquiry concerning reports of UFO incidents
made in his West Wales constituency.

4. Copy of RAF Police report on their investigation into reports of unidentified low
flying aircraft and sonic booms reported near Sheffield, Yorkshire, on 24 March
1997.

The existence of the report referred to in item (4) was confirmed in a letter I received
from BEfeetel of Sec(AS)2a, dated 4 January 2000. He said although RAF police
reports were ‘classified’ he was able to provide a summary of its content. However, under
the Code I have already successfully applied for and received copies of RAF Police
reports with personal details deleted. As no RAF/NATO aircraft were identified during
the investigation and no further action taken I now wish to apply for a copy of the full
investigation file under the term of the Code.

Thank you for your attention to my requests.

Yours sincerely,

a eee “Sena
S228 Anche
13.06.05

Dear Dr Clarke
1. Further to my letter of 30 January, please find enclosed the following documents:

The remaining papers requested from file DS8/75/3 Part A. I apologise for the length of
time it has taken to supply these documents which was due to the necessity to consult the
Foreign and Commonwealth Office about the sensitivity of the information they contain.
You will notice that the documents have been redacted in a different way to those
previously sent to you, with in most cases the top and bottom of the document being
removed. This is because although it is our practice to supply copies of documents

‘requested wherever possible, the Code of Practice on Access to Government Information

(the Code) (Part I paragraph 4) states “there is no commitment that pre-existing
documents, as distinct from information, will be made available in response to requests”.
In light of this, the FCO have requested that if we wish to supply copies of the documents,
rather than extracts, that we redact them in this way. The information removed concerns
addresses of Departments and individuals within the FCO, contacts with them, and copy
addressees.

A second copy of the document referred to in paragraph 2 of my letter dated 30 January
2003. As we have now been able to release the documents mentioned above, I can also
supply the information in paragraph 5, lines 7 to 9 of this paper which were previously
withheld.

A copy of an MP’s letter to the Secretary of State dated 24 October 1996, the MOD
response to which was sent to you with my letter of 9 December 2002.

Finally, thank you for your letter of 20 February in which you offer assistance with our
review of UFO records. We recognise the public interest in UFO related records and are
keen to add further UFO classes of information to the MOD FOI Publication Scheme. We
will therefore, shortly be consulting with our colleagues in various branches including the
Defence Intelligence staff, the Directorate of Air Operations, and those with responsibility
for records management and the Publication Scheme, to decide what material could be
released into the Scheme. While this is likely to be a significant task, we believe that the

Department has sufficient staff with the necessary expertise to undertake this work,
without external assistance. However, thank you for your interest and should the situation
change, we will certainly take your offer into consideration.

I hope this is helpful.

Yours sincerely,

ESTABLISHMENT OF AN AGENCY OR A DEPARTMENT OF THE UNITED
NATIONS For UNDERTAKING, CO-ORDINATING AND DISSEMINATING
THE RESULTS OF RESEARCH INTO UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS
AND RELATED PHENOMENA (ITEM PROPOSED BY GRENADA (S/33/131)).

The attached brief has been prepared by the Energy Science and

Space Department in the Foreign and Commonwealth Office in
consultation with Departmant of Industry and the Home Office.
Any comments Should be addressed to D A Lloyd in Energy
Science ang Space

Department, Foreign and Commonwealth Office,
Tel 233 3209. :

~UNGLASSIRIED
“A UK AIMS

To oppose the establishment of any such agency.

B POINTS TO MAKE

2. The British delegation does not think that the establish-
ment of an agency for research into unidentified flying
objects is appropriate to the functions of the United Nations,
It considers that the existence of such an agency under UN
auspices would be an unjustifiable drain on UN
which could be better deployed elsewhere.

resources

Cc TACTICS

3. Hopefully a confrontation “ith the representative of
Grenada can be avoided, but the UK should not hesitate to

make its views known as and when appropriate. You should

seek whatever Support you can particulsrly among the Nine.
BACKGROUND

4. At last year's UN General Assembly, the Grenadan
delegation, with the full Support of Premier Gairy, sought
to establish an ad hoc working group of the UN to evaluate
UFO reports. Fay ilinsiters expressed the view that to set
up any such body would reduces the credibility of the UN;
accordingly the UK delegation was instructed to oppdése.
In the event, no vote was taken on the Grenadan Resolution;
aS & compromise, the General Assembly "studied the text"

and reguested the secretary-General to transmit it "to states
members of the UN and to interested Specialised agencies so

that they might communicate their views to the Secretary-
General",

UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS

1. You asked me to comment on loose minute DCD3(I)/52 dated 9 December 1977,

As you know, S4(Air) are responsible for answering public enquiries and for briefing
Ministers on MPs' questions about UFOs. I attach our stock reply which has been
endorsed by successive Hinisters.— dul Aerek , Pad Opes , tag gine te Ute Grdeo Gee
apy CF CDS 4 Cone pyri,

2. So far as I know there has been no British Scientific enquiry into the possible
existence of UFOs, nor is there evidence that MOD has taken the question very
seriously. However, we have kept in touch with the United States Air Force who

made a systematic, if protracted, attempt to record and investigate UFO sightings
from 1952 to 1966. The USAF enquiry, know as’ Project Blue Book, was followed

by a study which was originally suggested by a member of a congressional investigation
in the Armed Forces Committee and which was assigned by the Air Force Office of
Seientific Research to the University of Colorado. It was led by Dr Edward ¥ Condon,
Professor of Physics and formerly Director of the National Bureau of Standards.

This is the enquiry referred to in the attached brief and, as stated there, it

was endorsed in 1969 by the National Academy of Sciences who concluded that there
was no scientific or military justification for further study of UFOs, that the
reports of UFOs up to that time posed no threat to national security and that
continuance of the Blue Book Project was of doubtful value. The Condon report
caused a good deal of controversy and, I believe, was much criticised. However,

when I consulted the RAF Staff in Washington about this they assured me in

August 1976 that HQ USAF saw no likelihood of their further involvement in this

area. The USAF pointed to the considerable Air Force commitment of resources in

the past, the extreme pressure on Air Force funds in 1976 and the fact that since
1969 no evidence had been presented to warrant further investigation.

3. It is always possible of course, that the considerable correspondence addressed
to MOD might contain some hint, not of Ur0s, but of something with a hostile
terrestrial origin which could have a bearing on national security. We have
therefore been careful to refer all UFO enquiries from the public to ADI5S and DD

Ops (GE)(RAF) for any further investigation which they might consider to be justified,
As a Secretariat branch, S4(Air) does not need to know about this type of enquiry

and we do not ask for further information; we feat the public flatly that we

cannot enter into detailed correspondence, andjenquiries are not necessarily made

to the point of positive identification. The only exception to this is the

Occasions) cace wharn a Monkan af Dawtinmont chore ovaeattansl f:

wnat ay

and we seek ADI 55's advice so that the Minister can reply more fully and
specifically than the standsrd draft allows.

4. That is all there really gisto the MOD UFO "policy" egept that we do not
publish the correspondence frog the public. ‘The only reason for that is lack
of staff; we would have to obliterate the identitics of ovr many correspondents,
and possibly occasional references to classificd subjects, and there is no one
we can spare for the job.
REDACTION ON ORIGINAL DOCUMENT

5. As it happens, however, I was about to seek confirmation of the general line
we take even before you consulted me on the letter sent to CDS. There is always
a steady public interest in UFOs and from time to time it tends to increase
unaccountably. There was a sustained flurry in the 1960s which was sufficient to
embroil my predecessor in a BBC TV programme. Last summer there was some concern
in Wales although the RAF Police thought this could have been the work of a practical
joker. In the last few weeks the Prime Minister of Grenada has secured a UFO
debate at the United Nations (which could concewably involve. the Foreign Office
and No 10 in correspondence) and he has also had discussions with Mr Jimmy Carter
whom the Press have connected with UFOs in the past. Then a few days ago the
Earl of Glancarty put down a number of questions in the House of Lords referring
to UFO investigations allegedly under way in France and a broadcast said to have

been made in 1974 by the Contemporary French Minister of Defence. (See Hansard
extracts attached).

6. We believe that

, is a Vice President of the British UFO Research Association. I should
add that we were unable to verify the alleged Ministerial broadcast when a member
of the public told us in 1976 that in it the French had admitted the existence
of flying saucers. The British Air Attache in Paris did find out however that the
French operate a procedure under which UFO reports from the public go in the first
instance to the French Defence Ministry (Air) who study the military implications
and finally to a Government organisation known as the Centre Nationale D'Etude
Spatial where the reports are considered by scientists. The reports are also
fed into a computer where they are "added to the statistics of the problem",
We were advised that the French military authorities had found nothing of an
aggressive nature in the sightings although their scientists had been unable to
explain the phenomena. As this information came to us by telephone I have now
written to the Air Attache to document it and up date it in the light of the Marl
of Glancarty's questions.

7. One way and another there could be renewed interest in UFOs in the next
few months and I have already been asked to take stock of our current policy.
The basic questions are: -

a. Should we take flying saucers seriously?

bo. Is the general MOD policy valid (as outlined in the attached brief and
as supplemented above)?

Ce Is it correct to confine MOD investigations to the defence implications
of alleged UFO sightings - should the investigations be extended either in
the interest of national defence or more widely into the more speculative
field concerning the possible existence of extra terrestrial ,vehicles?

d. Should MOD take the public more seriously and, for example, visit the
places where sightings have been reported (a line of enquiry; I might add
which could not be undertaken by S4(Air)'s slender establishment)?

8. I should be most grateful if you would seek the views of DSTI and Air
Cdre (int) whom I believe you will be consulting before reporting to CDS.

vearetick a)

I am very concerned about an incident that occurred off the
East Anglian coast recently, involving a visual unidentified
flying craft sighting which was correlated by various different
military radar systems. I have attached, for your information,
copies of some recent press reports.

From these press reports it would seem as if the unidentified
flying craft was seen by the crew of a tanker; the crew of a civil
aircraft; police at Skegness (who took a video), and police at
Boston. Simultaneously, the object seems to have been picked up
on military radar systems at R.A.F. Neatishead, R.A.F. Waddington,

and R.A.F. Northwood, together with systems’ at London and at
Anglia radar.

What strikes me as incredible is that no aircraft were
scrambled when an uncorrelated target was picked up so close to
the coast. This raises for me, serious issues about the way in
which we police the U.K. Air Defence Region (U.K.A.D.R.). Given
that we have Tornado F.3 aircraft based at R.A.F. Leuchars; R.A.F.
Leeming; and R.A.F. Coningsby, should not one of these bases have
had Air Defence aircraft on Quick Reaction Alert (Q.R.A.), and
should a launch not have been ordered? I am also unclear as to
the involvement of R.A.F. Kinloss in Scotland, home of our Nimrod
Maritime Patrol Aircraft.

A story seems to have been put around that the radar systems
were picking up Boston”’s church tower! Although "ground clutter"
can give spurious returns around certain radar heads, my
understanding is that the locations of such areas are weil known,
and that such an effect is unlikely to be repeated on so many
different radar systems. Some of the Air Traffic Control radars
might have difficulty with ground clutter, but Air Defence radar
systems and their skilled operators should know the difference.

This "explanation" also fails to take account of the visual
sightings.

/Cont. 2

-?2- Ae
REDACTION ON ORIGINAL DOCUMENT

HOUSE OF COMMONS
LONDON SWIA OAA

Naturally, all sorts of rumours are circulating: no Q.R.A.
aircraft were available at R.A.F. Coningsby, which is just a few
miles from the Wash; the R.A.F. wanted to scramble aircraft but
were overruled "at the highest level"; the video taken by the
police has disappeared into the bowels of your Ministry”s Main
Building. While I am interested in finding out what was seen, my
primary concern stems from the absolute shambles that such events
seem to cause, The R.A.F. are supposed, or so I believed, to be
responsible for keeping a watchful eye on activity in the
U.K.A.D.R., but seem to have no idea as to what is going on. Do
they have no standard procedure for such incidents? They had
enough time to think about it, because the object was on radar for
upwards of seven hours! These concerns remain, even if there is
a prosaic explanation for this specific incident.

I could think of countless other questions that concern me
based on the points I have raised in this letter and in the
various press reports, but I will confine myself, in addition to
the above, if you will answer the following questions in respect
of the unidentified flying craft sighting that occurred on 4th./
5th. October in the vicinity of the Wash, and subsequently
reported to your Department:

1. If you will detail the role played by those military
establishments who picked up on their radar systems or were
otherwise involved in events, and if you will comment on the

video of the sighting sent to your Department by the Boston
police?

2. Why no aircraft were scrambled to investigate the
unidentified flying craft seen by the police; the crew of a
civil aircraft; and the crew of a tanker on 4th./5th. October,
given that at least three military establishments reported
having detected the unidentified flying craft on radar?

3. Whether it is the R.A.F’s standard practice to ignore
sightings of unidentified flying craft which are correlated by

radar, or whether there is a requirement to investigate such
phenomena by scrambling aircraft?

I look forward to your reply with great interest.

Yours sincerely,

This Is the transcript of the conversations between
coastguards, RAF stations In Norfolk, Scotland and
London, police and North Sea tanker Conocoast
when strange lIghts were seen In the night sky at
the weekend. It Is the recording taken at
Yarmouth Coastguard headquarters:

red-and-green rotating light in the sky directly

south-east from Skegness. It looks strange as it .

is stationary and there is no aircraft sound in
the area.”

0326 RAF Kinloss: “Northwood have a radar
contact bearing 221 degrees at 16 miles, it looks
to be stationary and there is no way of
determining its height but it must be quite a
size to be visible from Skegness.”

couple of radar contacts in the area but no
height, they seen to be stationary. There are
definitely no military aircraft in the area and
no notified civil flights should be there.”

on visual. Now they are flashing red, green and
white. Cannot identify it as an aircraft as it
looks stationary and it is approximately one
mile high.” .

direction it appeared?”

Conocoast: “No. It just appeared and is
stationary.”

caused by the weather”

contact.”

It is obvious that whatever it is it does not want

flashing red, green, blue and white. It looks
very high, north of us, and there is no engine

green and red.”

two lights”

deg true and the other at 160 true, the lights are
both visible with the naked eye and both

Did you see the mysterious bright lights?

The source of “strange” bright
lights spotted in the sky off East
Anglia at the weekend remained a
mystery last night.

Police, the RAF and shipping
reported the appearance of two
large, unexplained objects above
the Wash in the early hours of
Saturday.

Baffled . personnel at RAF
Neatishead spent hours tracking
the UFOs, described by witnesses
as large, about a mile up in the sky,
and with blue, white, red and green
flashing lights. —~

The Ministry of Defence said it

was taking the sightings seriously
and “all possibilities” would be
covered.

Spokesman Nigel Sergeant said:
“We are trying to prove that it does
not represent any sort of security
threat and that it was not an
aggressive intrusion into our

- airspace. This is one of the bigger

sightings recently and has caused
quite a bit of interest.”

Skegness police called the
coastguards at 3.14am on Saturday
after seeing a number of objects
flashing in the sky, which were also
spotted by colleagues at Boston.

if WITH everyone’s minds
on strange things in the sky
(Evening News, October 7)
now seems a good time to

ask whether anyone else saw
the silent object I did over
the centre of Norwich at
around 9am on December 27
last year, a date and time
which meant the city was
virtually empty.

It certainly wasn’t a flying
saucer, more a brightly
coloured flying object, and
appeared at first to be a giant
kite about the dimensions of
a single-decker bus.

As the fair was here at the
time it might well have been
a publicity stunt to attract
attention. It might have been
any number of things — but

quite what is the puzzle.
Malcolm Chamberlin,

Highland Road,
Norwich.

The great East Anglian UFO mystery was ee

solved last night -— ‘and turned out to be just a

storm and a church tower.

‘The weekend sightings of colourful Hashing ereat W ash
lights over the Wash captured the imagination

of would-be X-Files sleuths across the region. ~

Letters and telephone calls flooded into the

EDP after the sightings, which coincided with

the appearance of a mysterious object’on

radars. . .

One theory was that the culprit was planet h

Venus. . Si £ tl n gS

But Norfolk RAF investigators have now

found logical explanations - and have ruled

.
out the possibility of little green men peering f ll
down on us. ina Y
Flight Lt Keith Sweatman, of RAF Neatishead,

| said: “We now know that the radar trace was

“You do get weird experiences with electrical

storms, and they can produce lots of different

colours,”

The 200ft church tower would have been ruled

advanced RAF radar, which can computer-

enhance images.

But the air traffic control radar did not have

the tlashing red, blue, green and white lights,

seen by the tanker Conocoast.

Following the weekend sightings, an

discounted. .

Your ref: D/DAS/64/3/11
20 February, 2003

was ection 40

Thank you for your letter of 30 January 2003 and for enclosing the copies of the
additional UFO-related papers I had requested, arising from the Parliamentary
Correspondence file.

lam grateful for the detailed answers you provided to the additional points I raised, and I
now await your decision on the release of.a) the remaining papers from file DS8/75/3
Part A and b) the letter from the late Martin Redmond MP in file D/Sec(AS)64/2.

Jn the meantime, with reference to my inquiry about the future of UFO records that are
less than 30 years old, I note that you “will be reviewing the information we hold to see
what material may be made more generally available via the MOD Freedom of
Information Publication Scheme.”

Your commitment to make material more freely available via the Internet is
commendable. As you no doubt appreciate, there is continuing public, media and
academic interest in these records, as reflected in their inclusion in the MOD Publication
Scheme announced in the Lord Chancellor’s Department Press Notice 424/02 (28
November 2002).

The early release of this material is undoubtedly the most effective way to dispel
misconceptions about the Ministry’s limited involvement in this area, and to demonstrate
the FOIA working in practice. However, I do appreciate that the reviewing of records to
remove personal information has always posed a major logistical problem. Given your
Secretariat’s limited time and resources, such matters quite understandably remain a low
priority given more pressing defence concerns.

I understand that your ‘UFO’ records are currently split between three locations: a) those
awaiting release at the PRO, b) those at the ‘midway position’ in MOD archives and c)
those most recent files retained in your office. If you are serious about your intention to
review these records so that additional material could be made available from 2005, a
detailed inventory of the content of existing ‘UFO’ files in all three locations would be
necessary. For this to be achieved, I would suggest that such a review would be more
effective with input from a professional historian who has a specific interest in and a
proven track record of peer-reviewed research in this area.

You will be aware that for some time I have been conducting historical research into the
MOD’s involvement in ‘UFO’ matters, as part of my post-doctoral research based at the
National Centre for English Cultural Tradition, University of Sheffield. My work has
involved a very detailed study of material available at the Public Record Office dating
from 1947 to 1972, and more recently via applications to your Secretariat for access to
files closed under the current ’30 year rule’ under the Code of Practice.

My research has now progressed to the stage where I am preparing an application to the
Economic and Social Research Council for a funded, two year project. This will propose
an extension to my research base, resulting in the production of a detailed study of MOD
investigations from 1950 to present. This research would be of interest to MOD both in
terms of providing a comprehensive history of its involvement in this area, and as a
reference work for use in future years. Furthermore, as the E.S.R.C. operate a joint
funding scheme with MOD whereby collaboration is encouraged with research
institutions on “defence issues and other areas which may be of interest to the MOD” this
underscores the timely nature of my proposal.

My proposal has the support of a number of prominent ee
a”

who are providing
references. In addition, I would point out that MOD assured the Under Secretary of State
for Air as long ago as 1970 that “if a major scientific organisation of high standing had
strong reasons for obtaining access to our records their application would be considered
on its merits.” (Head of S4 Air to US of S, 10 February 1970 in PRO AIR 25/1556)

Given MOD’s renewed commitment to “reviewing...information we hold to see what
material may be made more generally available”, I wish to offer assistance in the review
and preparation of ‘UFO’ material for release under the FOIA. This work, including
clerical assistance, could be undertaken as part of my funding proposal to E.S.R.C. with
no additional cost to MOD.

I hope you will consider this proposal on its merits, and to that end I enclose a formal
letter in support from the Director of NATCECT, Dr Joan Beal, at my University.

Yours sincerely,

Dr David Clarke
